_source line 4696 · 2026-05-19T19:03:19.661Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_018Mr6wz5R3fpGisexHDo63T`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Large language models deployed as interactive agents must repeatedly decide \emph{how} to compute: reason internally, or delegate to an external tool.
4	In ScienceWorld \cite{scienceworld-2022}, a text-based science simulation, internal reasoning is error-prone---the model may hallucinate state changes (e.g., that water has boiled when the stove was never activated)---while deterministic external computation eliminates such errors via exact state transitions. The practical question: \emph{when should an agent route computation externally versus internally?}
5	
6	Existing routing approaches frame this as a capability or efficiency decision: Toolformer \cite{toolformer-2023} inserts API calls by perplexity reduction; ART \cite{art-2023} decomposes tasks into tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \cite{chameleon-2023} selects heterogeneous modules per subtask; adaptive-computation methods route to different reasoning depths \cite{cogrouter-2026,ares-2026} or model tiers \cite{mtrouter-2026}; concurrent analyses further presuppose diverse routing signals~\citep{when2tool-2026,tool-use-tax-2026,to-call-or-not-2026}. The shared assumption---that routing signal stems from diverse sources and a learned router can exploit it---has not been tested: no prior work has decomposed what drives routing decisions in interactive environments.
7	
8	We propose a \emph{diagnostic framework} comprising (i)~episode-level counterfactual labeling under pure strategies (\S\ref{sec:collection}--\ref{sec:labeling}), and (ii)~the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF), which decomposes the routing score gap into death-avoidance and non-death components (\S\ref{sec:daf}). Applied to ScienceWorld, \textbf{78.9\% of non-tie routing labels arise from death asymmetry} (75 of 95), a penalty-independent finding. Under ScienceWorld's penalty ($P{=}100$), DAF reaches $90.4\%$ (95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$). The internal strategy's hallucination-induced failures trigger catastrophic death ($-100$ penalty); once death episodes are removed, the gap collapses to $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$ (\S\ref{sec:death-decomp}).
9	
10	This addresses a methodological gap: per-step oracle comparison degenerates under sparse rewards (97.4\% tie rate; \S\ref{sec:collection}), and keyword-based heuristics fail structurally (\S\ref{sec:baselines}). Our approach runs complete episodes under pure-internal (ReAct \cite{react-2023}) and pure-deterministic strategies, comparing episode outcomes.
11	
12	Cross-context checks probe specificity: MATH Level~4--5 ($N{=}795$, code interpreter) shows no routing signal without catastrophic failure ($73.5\%$ vs.\ $73.0\%$, $p = 0.81$); Llama-3.1-8B replication ($N{=}220$) confirms death dominance across model families (direction consistency 10/10; \S\ref{sec:cross-model}). Four intervention axes (retry, Reflexion~\citep{reflexion-2023}, $10{\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction) reduce death rates but none closes the gap to deterministic safety, with ${\geq}$12~pp residual gaps (\S\ref{sec:agent-improvement}).
13	An exploratory router (Qwen3 LoRA, 0.6B--4B) yields flat step-level balanced accuracy ($56$--$59\%$); at episode level, only 4B exceeds the always-deterministic baseline (\S\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).
14	
15	Our contributions: (1)~an \textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} that overcomes per-step oracle degeneracy (97.4\% tie rate) and produces three interpretable outputs: penalty-independent label fractions, a Death Avoidance Factor decomposition, and oracle degeneracy detection (\S\ref{sec:collection}--\ref{sec:daf}), enabling practitioners to assess routing value from a ${\sim}$50-episode pilot before investing in router training; (2)~applying this framework to ScienceWorld, we find that \textbf{78.9\% of non-tie routing labels} stem from death asymmetry---a single mechanism dominates routing signal (\S\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\ref{sec:agent-improvement}); (3)~\textbf{cross-model validation} (Llama-3.1-8B, DAF${=}88.2\%$), four intervention axes, and cross-environment boundary probes (MATH, ALFWorld) confirm the framework's diagnostic utility and the robustness of the finding (\S\ref{sec:math-contrast}--\ref{sec:cross-model}).
16	
17	
18	
```

### Tool result

_source line 4697 · 2026-05-19T19:03:19.936Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01WYTCPYYw5t2Zqx73CxD1Sm`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Per-step oracle comparison degenerates under sparse rewards---in ScienceWorld, 97.4\% of steps yield identical scores regardless of computation mode. We introduce an episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework that overcomes this degeneracy by decomposing routing signal into death-avoidance and non-death components via paired pure-strategy episodes. Applying this framework to ScienceWorld (11 task types, 245 episodes, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), we find that \textbf{78.9\% of non-tie routing labels} stem from death asymmetry---a penalty-independent result; a derived measure (DAF) reaches 90.4\% at $P{=}100$. Cross-model replication (Llama-3.1-8B, DAF${=}88.2\%$), four intervention axes (retry, self-correction, $10{\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction), and boundary probes against contrasting environments (MATH, ALFWorld) confirm the framework's diagnostic utility and the robustness of this finding. The framework diagnoses when catastrophic failure makes routing signal degenerate, enabling practitioners to assess routing value before investing in router training.
3	\end{abstract}
4	
```

    "oldString": "Our contributions: (1)~an \\textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} that overcomes per-step oracle degeneracy (97.4\\% tie rate) and produces three interpretable outputs: penalty-independent label fractions, a Death Avoidance Factor decomposition, and oracle degeneracy detection (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:daf}), enabling practitioners to assess routing value from a ${\\sim}$50-episode pilot before investing in router training; (2)~applying this framework to ScienceWorld, we find that \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels} stem from death asymmetry---a single mechanism dominates routing signal (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\\ref{sec:agent-improvement});",
    "newString": "Our contributions: (1)~an \\textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} that overcomes per-step oracle degeneracy (97.4\\% tie rate), decomposes routing signal via penalty-independent (label fraction) and penalty-dependent (DAF) metrics, and predicts routing value from a ${\\sim}$50-episode pilot (validated 4/4 conditions; Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}); (2)~applying this framework to ScienceWorld, we find that \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels} stem from death asymmetry---a single mechanism that persists across four intervention axes and three model scales, contradicting the routing literature's implicit assumption of diverse signal sources (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\\ref{sec:agent-improvement});",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nLarge language models deployed as interactive agents must repeatedly decide \\emph{how} to compute: reason internally, or delegate to an external tool.\nIn ScienceWorld \\cite{scienceworld-2022}, a text-based science simulation, internal reasoning is error-prone---the model may hallucinate state changes (e.g., that water has boiled when the stove was never activated)---while deterministic external computation eliminates such errors via exact state transitions. The practical question: \\emph{when should an agent route computation externally versus internally?}\n\nExisting routing approaches frame this as a capability or efficiency decision: Toolformer \\cite{toolformer-2023} inserts API calls by perplexity reduction; ART \\cite{art-2023} decomposes tasks into tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \\cite{chameleon-2023} selects heterogeneous modules per subtask; adaptive-computation methods route to different reasoning depths \\cite{cogrouter-2026,ares-2026} or model tiers \\cite{mtrouter-2026}; concurrent analyses further presuppose diverse routing signals~\\citep{when2tool-2026,tool-use-tax-2026,to-call-or-not-2026}. The shared assumption---that routing signal stems from diverse sources and a learned router can exploit it---has not been tested: no prior work has decomposed what drives routing decisions in interactive environments.\n\nWe propose a \\emph{diagnostic framework} comprising (i)~episode-level counterfactual labeling under pure strategies (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:labeling}), and (ii)~the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF), which decomposes the routing score gap into death-avoidance and non-death components (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}). Applied to ScienceWorld, \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels arise from death asymmetry} (75 of 95), a penalty-independent finding. Under ScienceWorld's penalty ($P{=}100$), DAF reaches $90.4\\%$ (95\\% CI: $[81.6\\%, 98.8\\%]$). The internal strategy's hallucination-induced failures trigger catastrophic death ($-100$ penalty); once death episodes are removed, the gap collapses to $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$ (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}).\n\nThis addresses a methodological gap: per-step oracle comparison degenerates under sparse rewards (97.4\\% tie rate; \\S\\ref{sec:collection}), and keyword-based heuristics fail structurally (\\S\\ref{sec:baselines}). Our approach runs complete episodes under pure-internal (ReAct \\cite{react-2023}) and pure-deterministic strategies, comparing episode outcomes.\n\nCross-context checks probe specificity: MATH Level~4--5 ($N{=}795$, code interpreter) shows no routing signal without catastrophic failure ($73.5\\%$ vs.\\ $73.0\\%$, $p = 0.81$); Llama-3.1-8B replication ($N{=}220$) confirms death dominance across model families (direction consistency 10/10; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}). Four intervention axes (retry, Reflexion~\\citep{reflexion-2023}, $10{\\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction) reduce death rates but none closes the gap to deterministic safety, with ${\\geq}$12~pp residual gaps (\\S\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}).\nAn exploratory router (Qwen3 LoRA, 0.6B--4B) yields flat step-level balanced accuracy ($56$--$59\\%$); at episode level, only 4B exceeds the always-deterministic baseline (\\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).\n\nOur contributions: (1)~an \\textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} that overcomes per-step oracle degeneracy (97.4\\% tie rate) and produces three interpretable outputs: penalty-independent label fractions, a Death Avoidance Factor decomposition, and oracle degeneracy detection (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:daf}), enabling practitioners to assess routing value from a ${\\sim}$50-episode pilot before investing in router training; (2)~applying this framework to ScienceWorld, we find that \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels} stem from death asymmetry---a single mechanism dominates routing signal (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}); (3)~\\textbf{cross-model validation} (Llama-3.1-8B, DAF${=}88.2\\%$), four intervention axes, and cross-environment boundary probes (MATH, ALFWorld) confirm the framework's diagnostic utility and the robustness of the finding (\\S\\ref{sec:math-contrast}--\\ref{sec:cross-model}).\n\n\n",

Four intervention axes (retry, Reflexion~\\citep{reflexion-2023}, $10{\\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction) reduce death rates but none closes the gap to deterministic safety, with ${\\geq}$12~pp residual gaps (\\S\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}).",

[Director 指令] **[Patch] 2 个 critical 修复 + 2 个 warning 修复**

Reviewer 深度审查发现数据一致性问题，必须立即修复。

**Critical 1：DAF 95% CI 全文统一**
当前论文有三处引用 DAF CI，数值不统一：
- §3.1 experiments.tex L17: "[81.6%, 98.8%], B=10,000" ← 旧值，声称 B=10,000 但实际来自 B=1,000
- main.tex L417 附近（Appendix 某处）: "[81.6%, 98.8%]" 标注 "1,000 resamples"
- Appendix K 表格 main.tex: "[81.1%, 99.0%], B=10,000" ← 正确的 recomputed 值

操作：
1. §3.1 L17 的 CI 改为 [81.1%, 99.0%]（与 Appendix K recomputed 一致）
2. main.tex L417 附近：改为 B=10,000 和 [81.1%, 99.0%]
3. 全文 grep "[81.6" 和 "98.8"，确保没有其他残留的旧 CI（Introduction、Conclusion 等）

**Critical 2：§3.1 L26 CI 归属错误**
当前 L26："Non-clustered episode-level bootstrap (B = 10,000) yields 95% CI [81.6%, 98.8%] for the death-avoidance **label fraction**"
问题：label fraction 点估计是 78.9%，不在 [81.1%, 99.0%] 区间内。这个 CI 是 **DAF**（90.4%）的 CI。
修正：改为 "...yields 95\% CI $[81.1\%, 99.0\%]$ for **DAF**"（或 "for the Death Avoidance Factor"）。如果 label fraction 有自己的 CI，可以分别列出。

**Warning 1：Abstract DAF 首次出现未展开**
Abstract 中首次出现 "(DAF)" 需展开为 "Death Avoidance Factor (DAF)"。Abstract 应独立可读。

**Warning 2：cross-model routing transfer 未引用**
实验 cross-model-guided-router-eval 发现 Qwen routing rules 迁移到 Llama = chance accuracy。这直接支撑 "death vulnerability is a task-environment property"。在 §3.2 Multi-Model Validation 末尾加一句，类似："Cross-model routing transfer confirms this structural interpretation: per-task routing rules derived from Qwen yield chance accuracy on Llama, indicating that routing signal is environment-determined rather than model-specific (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model})."
注意：需确认这个数据在 Appendix 中有具体数字可引用。如果没有，只加正文一句不加 appendix ref。

修完后编译确认。全文 grep "81.6" 和 "98.8" 确保无残留。

414	
415	Augmentation disproportionately increases the majority (deterministic) class: Det\% rises from 89.6\% at 100\% to 94.2\% at 200\% and ${\sim}97.7\%$ at 800\%. However, the complete collapse to majority-class prediction (internal recall $= 0.0$) occurs even at the smallest unaugmented scale (25\%, ${\sim}90\%$ det); this is not an augmentation artifact. Balanced accuracy corrects for class imbalance by averaging per-class recall, so the metric itself is not biased by the skew.
416	
417	Decomposition metrics are stable under bootstrap resampling (1{,}000 resamples of the 245-episode dataset): DAF $= 90.6\% \pm 4.2\%$ (95\% CI $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$); Pearson $r = 0.975 \pm 0.011$ (95\% CI $[0.952, 0.995]$). The death avoidance dominance finding is robust to both resampling and split perturbation.
418	
419	\paragraph{Per-task-type evaluation breakdown.} To test whether the router learns transferable routing strategies or merely task-type classification, we evaluate existing checkpoints (8 seeds) separately on each of the 11 task types. Of the 11 task types, 7 have \emph{all-deterministic} labels (no internal-preferred episodes exist), making balanced accuracy undefined; the router has nothing to learn beyond the trivial always-deterministic default. For the remaining 4 task types where both classes are present, balanced accuracy ranges from 50.0\% to 73.0\%, but this apparent signal does not indicate cross-task-type routing transfer: the router is effectively classifying task types (which correlate with death risk) rather than extracting a generalizable routing strategy. This complements the data scaling ablation: routing signal does not transfer across task types because the signal \emph{is} the task type, i.e., whether a given task carries death risk. The 4B capacity scaling result (Appendix~\ref{app:ablations}) suggests capacity is one factor, but even 4B's $59.0\%$ episode-level accuracy does not demonstrate cross-task-type transfer.
420	
421	\section{Penalty Sensitivity Analysis}
422	\label{app:penalty}
423	
424	\begin{table}[h]
425	\centering
426	\small
427	\begin{tabular}{@{}rrr@{}}
428	\toprule
429	$P$ & DAF($P$) & 95\% CI \\
430	\midrule
431	0 & 7.4\% & [$-39.4$, $82.5$] \\
432	1 & 14.8\% & [$-28.6$, $89.6$] \\
433	5 & 35.4\% & [$-1.8$, $92.7$] \\

442	\caption{Sensitivity of DAF to death penalty magnitude $P$. DAF$(P) = 1 - \Delta_{\text{nd}} / \Delta_{\text{full}}(P)$, where $\Delta_{\text{full}}(P) \approx \delta \cdot P + \Delta_{\text{nd}}$, $\delta = 0.2653$ is the death-rate differential, and $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = 2.84$ (strict neither-died filtering). Recomputed from 245 episode-level data; bootstrap 95\% CIs ($B{=}10{,}000$). \textsuperscript{*}Default setting.}

527	\label{app:cross-model}
528	
529	We replicate the counterfactual analysis with Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct across all 11 task types ($N{=}220$ episode pairs, 20 per type; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-detail}).
530	The internal strategy dies in $41.4\%$ of episodes versus $11.4\%$ for deterministic (death-rate differential $+30.0$~pp), with 83 deterministic-preferred, 3 internal-preferred (1.4\%, all death-asymmetry reversals), and 134 no-preference labels.
531	Both models exhibit nearly identical internal vulnerability (${\sim}$42\%: Qwen $42.9\%$, Llama $41.4\%$), confirming that death-prone failure is structurally determined. The differential magnitude varies ($+26.5$~pp Qwen, $+30.0$~pp Llama) because deterministic strategy safety is model-dependent ($16.3\%$ vs.\ $11.4\%$), but directional consistency is 100\% (10/10 death-prone tasks in both models).
532	\texttt{identify-life-stages-1} shows cross-model precision matching ($55\%$/$10\%$/$+45$~pp in both models).
533	\texttt{find-non-living-thing} shows zero deaths in both models, consistent with its majority-class answer distribution.

536	\centering

538	\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrl@{}}
539	\toprule
540	Task & Int\% & Det\% & $\Delta$\,pp & D / I / N \\

542	identify-life-stages-1 & 55 & 10 & +45 & 9 / 0 / 11 \\
543	freeze & 45 & 0 & +45 & 9 / 0 / 11 \\
544	grow-fruit & 45 & 5 & +40 & 8 / 0 / 12 \\

546	measure-melting-point & 50 & 15 & +35 & 9 / 0 / 11 \\
547	lifespan-longest & 35 & 5 & +30 & 7 / 1 / 12 \\
548	lifespan-l-then-s & 50 & 20 & +30 & 6 / 0 / 14 \\
549	chemistry-mix & 45 & 20 & +25 & 5 / 0 / 15 \\
550	inclined-plane & 45 & 20 & +25 & 6 / 0 / 14 \\
551	find-animal & 40 & 25 & +15 & 6 / 2 / 12 \\
552	find-non-living-thing & 0 & 0 & 0 & 10 / 0 / 10 \\
553	\midrule
554	\textbf{Aggregate} & \textbf{41.4} & \textbf{11.4} & \textbf{+30.0} & \textbf{83 / 3 / 134} \\
555	\bottomrule
556	\end{tabular}
557	\caption{Per-task Llama-3.1-8B cross-model replication ($N{=}20$ per task). D/I/N: deterministic-preferred / internal-preferred / no-preference. Sorted by $\Delta$ DR descending.}
558	\label{tab:cross-model-detail}
559	\end{table}
560	
561	\section{Failure Mode Analysis}
562	\label{app:failure-modes}
563	
564	All 105 internal-strategy deaths (100\%) are triggered by the same action pattern: \texttt{focus on [object]}. In ScienceWorld, \texttt{focus on} is the answer-submission action (equivalent to ``this is my final answer''); the LLM agent systematically misuses it as a general interaction or exploration command, and applies it to irrelevant targets (rooms, doors, inventory items). Each such execution submits an incorrect answer and triggers the $-100$ death penalty. Within death episodes, both strategies exhibit comparable \texttt{focus on} rates (${\sim}5\%$); the internal strategy's higher death count (105 vs.\ 40 for deterministic) reflects how often each strategy enters catastrophic states, not different action selection within those states.
565	
566	Manual inspection identifies four failure categories: (A) no task awareness, where the agent executes \texttt{focus on} without reading the task; (B) task-aware but lacking action grounding, where the agent correctly identifies the objective but cannot map it to valid action primitives; (D) genuine attempts with execution failure; and (E) initial progress wasted by early errors. Category B dominates: agents demonstrate task comprehension but systematically fail to translate it into correct ScienceWorld actions. This taxonomy was produced by a single annotator; no inter-annotator agreement was computed. The categories are intended as qualitative illustrations, not as formal labels with reliability guarantees.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01W7jmAWVYTdnW9W4k67fhk2`</summary>

```text
14	
15	Of the 95 non-tie routing labels, 75 (78.9\%) arise from death asymmetry: the internal strategy triggers a catastrophic $-100$ penalty while the deterministic strategy survives. This penalty-independent label fraction is the primary diagnostic metric. Of these 75, 54 are sole-signal labels (both strategies score zero without death, so death avoidance was the \emph{sole} discriminating signal). At $P{=}0$, the 54 sole-signal labels reduce to ties, leaving 41 non-tie labels of which 21 (51.2\%) still involve death asymmetry; the labeling function itself is penalty-independent (Algorithm~\ref{alg:labeling}), but the practical routing value of death avoidance requires $P > 0$.
16	
17	Of 245 episodes, 150 (61.2\%) are ties---neither strategy outperforms the other---indicating that routing is relevant in only 38.8\% of episodes. Among these, death avoidance dominates. The Death Avoidance Factor, a derived penalty-dependent measure, quantifies death's contribution to the total performance gap: DAF $= 90.4\%$ at $P{=}100$ ($\Delta_{\text{full}} = +29.60$, $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$; 95\% CI: $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$, episode-level bootstrap, $B{=}10{,}000$). DAF varies with penalty magnitude ($50.4\%$ at $P{=}10$; $82.6\%$ at $P{=}50$; Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}), confirming that the 78.9\% label fraction provides the more robust characterization. When $\Delta_{\text{nd}} \to 0$, DAF $\to 1$ is algebraic necessity regardless of actual death dominance; conversely, when $\Delta_{\text{nd}} < 0$ (as in Reflexion $N{\geq}3$; \S\ref{sec:agent-improvement}), DAF exceeds 100\%, reflecting that death asymmetry more than accounts for the full gap.
18	
19	\paragraph{Per-task correlation structure.}
20	The internal strategy dies in 42.9\% of episodes versus 16.3\% for deterministic (2.6$\times$), consistent with the internal strategy's reliance on self-generated observations that compound prediction errors across steps. Per-task, death rate differential correlates with $\Delta_{\text{full}}$ ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$; Figure~\ref{fig:decomposition}). Since $P$ is constant across all task types, $\Delta_{\text{full}} = P \cdot \delta + \Delta_{\text{nd}}$ makes $\Delta_{\text{full}}$ a linear function of $\delta$ with residual $\Delta_{\text{nd}}$; when DAF $\rightarrow 1$, this residual vanishes and $r \rightarrow 1$ by construction. The near-perfect correlation is therefore a mathematical consequence of death dominance, not an independent empirical finding. We retain it as an internal consistency check. The correlation drops below significance at $P \leq 25$ (Appendix~\ref{app:penalty}). Of 245 episodes, 86 are \texttt{det\_preferred}, 9 \texttt{internal\_preferred}, and 150 \texttt{no\_preference}.
21	
22	\paragraph{Signal concentration and binary structure.}
23	The Signal Concentration Index (SCI $= 0.2147$; Appendix~\ref{app:sci}) confirms low concentration: the top-3 task types account for only $39.8\%$ of total death-rate differential. The aggregate 78.9\% reflects a per-task binary structure: all 10 death-prone task types produce 100\% death-asymmetry labels, while \texttt{find-non-living-thing} (zero deaths, $\Delta = +9.58$ from non-death scores) produces 0\%. The framework thus diagnoses a binary property (``is this environment death-dominated?'') rather than a continuous routing signal. Removing \texttt{find-non-living-thing} raises the label fraction to 100\% and DAF to 96.8\% (Appendix~\ref{app:loto-daf}).
24	
25	\paragraph{Sensitivity and seed robustness.}
26	Non-clustered episode-level bootstrap ($B = 10{,}000$) yields 95\% CI $[81.6\%, 98.8\%]$ for the death-avoidance label fraction, independent of cluster assumptions, with Cohen's $d = 3.02$ (interpret cautiously given $K{=}11$ task types). Leave-one-task-out analysis confirms stability (label fraction range: 75.9\%--78.9\%; Appendix~\ref{app:loto-daf}); an independent seed replication ($N{=}220$) yields DAF $= 89.7\%$, within 0.7~pp (Appendix~\ref{app:seed-robustness}). As a conservative check, wild cluster bootstrap treating each task type as a cluster yields $p = 0.002$; however, with $K{=}11$ clusters this test falls below the $K{\geq}20$--$40$ threshold recommended for reliable inference~\citep{cameron-2008,webb-2023}, so we rely on the non-clustered bootstrap as primary. All 28 death-prone tasks share the same direction (Appendices~\ref{app:wild-cluster}--\ref{app:per-task-ci}).
27	
28	\subsection{Multi-Model Validation}
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0111KPFrQQxmp5QcmzWxCUJQ`</summary>

```text
28	\subsection{Multi-Model Validation}
29	\label{sec:cross-model}
30	
31	A Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{llama3-2024} cross-model replication across all 11 task types ($N{=}220$) confirms that death dominance replicates across model families: internal death rate $41.4\%$ vs.\ deterministic $11.4\%$ (death-rate differential $+30.0$~pp), DAF $= 88.2\%$ ($\Delta_{\text{full}} = +33.17$, $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +3.9$), with direction consistency 10/10 on death-prone tasks, 83 deterministic-preferred, 3 internal-preferred (1.4\%, all death-asymmetry reversals), and 134 no-preference labels.
32	Both models exhibit nearly identical internal vulnerability (${\sim}$42\%: Qwen $42.9\%$, Llama $41.4\%$), confirming that death-prone failure is structurally determined; the differential magnitude varies ($+26.5$~pp Qwen, $+30.0$~pp Llama) because deterministic strategy safety is model-dependent ($16.3\%$ vs.\ $11.4\%$).
33	\texttt{identify-life-stages-1} shows cross-model precision matching ($55\%$/$10\%$/$+45$~pp in both models).
34	\texttt{find-non-living-thing} shows zero deaths in both models, consistent with its majority-class answer distribution (\S\ref{sec:discussion}). Both models are in the 7--8B range; $10{\times}$ scaling (\S\ref{sec:agent-improvement}) confirms that death dominance persists across capability levels (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model}).
35

          " ",
          " Of the 95 non-tie routing labels, 75 (78.9\\%) arise from death asymmetry: the internal strategy triggers a catastrophic $-100$ penalty while the deterministic strategy survives. This penalty-independent label fraction is the primary diagnostic metric. Of these 75, 54 are sole-signal labels (both strategies score zero without death, so death avoidance was the \\emph{sole} discriminating signal). At $P{=}0$, the 54 sole-signal labels reduce to ties, leaving 41 non-tie labels of which 21 (51.2\\%) still involve death asymmetry; the labeling function itself is penalty-independent (Algorithm~\\ref{alg:labeling}), but the practical routing value of death avoidance requires $P > 0$.",
          " ",
          "-Of 245 episodes, 150 (61.2\\%) are ties---neither strategy outperforms the other---indicating that routing is relevant in only 38.8\\% of episodes. Among these, death avoidance dominates. The Death Avoidance Factor, a derived penalty-dependent measure, quantifies death's contribution to the total performance gap: DAF $= 90.4\\%$ at $P{=}100$ ($\\Delta_{\\text{full}} = +29.60$, $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$; 95\\% CI: $[81.6\\%, 98.8\\%]$, episode-level bootstrap, $B{=}10{,}000$). DAF varies with penalty magnitude ($50.4\\%$ at $P{=}10$; $82.6\\%$ at $P{=}50$; Appendix~\\ref{app:penalty}), confirming that the 78.9\\% label fraction provides the more robust characterization. When $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} \\to 0$, DAF $\\to 1$ is algebraic necessity regardless of actual death dominance; conversely, when $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} < 0$ (as in Reflexion $N{\\geq}3$; \\S\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}), DAF exceeds 100\\%, reflecting that death asymmetry more than accounts for the full gap.",
          "+Of 245 episodes, 150 (61.2\\%) are ties---neither strategy outperforms the other---indicating that routing is relevant in only 38.8\\% of episodes. Among these, death avoidance dominates. The Death Avoidance Factor, a derived penalty-dependent measure, quantifies death's contribution to the total performance gap: DAF $= 90.4\\%$ at $P{=}100$ ($\\Delta_{\\text{full}} = +29.60$, $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$; 95\\% CI: $[81.1\\%, 99.0\\%]$, episode-level bootstrap, $B{=}10{,}000$). DAF varies with penalty magnitude ($50.4\\%$ at $P{=}10$; $82.6\\%$ at $P{=}50$; Appendix~\\ref{app:penalty}), confirming that the 78.9\\% label fraction provides the more robust characterization. When $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} \\to 0$, DAF $\\to 1$ is algebraic necessity regardless of actual death dominance; conversely, when $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} < 0$ (as in Reflexion $N{\\geq}3$; \\S\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}), DAF exceeds 100\\%, reflecting that death asymmetry more than accounts for the full gap.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Per-task correlation structure.}",
          " The internal strategy dies in 42.9\\% of episodes versus 16.3\\% for deterministic (2.6$\\times$), consistent with the internal strategy's reliance on self-generated observations that compound prediction errors across steps. Per-task, death rate differential correlates with $\\Delta_{\\text{full}}$ ($r = 0.97$, $p < 10^{-6}$; Figure~\\ref{fig:decomposition}). Since $P$ is constant across all task types, $\\Delta_{\\text{full}} = P \\cdot \\delta + \\Delta_{\\text{nd}}$ makes $\\Delta_{\\text{full}}$ a linear function of $\\delta$ with residual $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}}$; when DAF $\\rightarrow 1$, this residual vanishes and $r \\rightarrow 1$ by construction. The near-perfect correlation is therefore a mathematical consequence of death dominance, not an independent empirical finding. We retain it as an internal consistency check. The correlation drops below significance at $P \\leq 25$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:penalty}). Of 245 episodes, 86 are \\texttt{det\\_preferred}, 9 \\texttt{internal\\_preferred}, and 150 \\texttt{no\\_preference}."

    "newString": "95\\% CI: $[81.1\\%, 99.0\\%]$",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nLarge language models deployed as interactive agents must repeatedly decide \\emph{how} to compute: reason internally, or delegate to an external tool.\nIn ScienceWorld \\cite{scienceworld-2022}, a text-based science simulation, internal reasoning is error-prone---the model may hallucinate state changes (e.g., that water has boiled when the stove was never activated)---while deterministic external computation eliminates such errors via exact state transitions. The practical question: \\emph{when should an agent route computation externally versus internally?}\n\nExisting routing approaches frame this as a capability or efficiency decision: Toolformer \\cite{toolformer-2023} inserts API calls by perplexity reduction; ART \\cite{art-2023} decomposes tasks into tool-augmented programs; Chameleon \\cite{chameleon-2023} selects heterogeneous modules per subtask; adaptive-computation methods route to different reasoning depths \\cite{cogrouter-2026,ares-2026} or model tiers \\cite{mtrouter-2026}; concurrent analyses further presuppose diverse routing signals~\\citep{when2tool-2026,tool-use-tax-2026,to-call-or-not-2026}. The shared assumption---that routing signal stems from diverse sources and a learned router can exploit it---has not been tested: no prior work has decomposed what drives routing decisions in interactive environments.\n\nWe propose a \\emph{diagnostic framework} comprising (i)~episode-level counterfactual labeling under pure strategies (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:labeling}), and (ii)~the Death Avoidance Factor (DAF), which decomposes the routing score gap into death-avoidance and non-death components (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}). Applied to ScienceWorld, \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels arise from death asymmetry} (75 of 95), a penalty-independent finding. Under ScienceWorld's penalty ($P{=}100$), DAF reaches $90.4\\%$ (95\\% CI: $[81.6\\%, 98.8\\%]$). The internal strategy's hallucination-induced failures trigger catastrophic death ($-100$ penalty); once death episodes are removed, the gap collapses to $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$ (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}).\n\nThis addresses a methodological gap: per-step oracle comparison degenerates under sparse rewards (97.4\\% tie rate; \\S\\ref{sec:collection}), and keyword-based heuristics fail structurally (\\S\\ref{sec:baselines}). Our approach runs complete episodes under pure-internal (ReAct \\cite{react-2023}) and pure-deterministic strategies, comparing episode outcomes.\n\nCross-context checks probe specificity: MATH Level~4--5 ($N{=}795$, code interpreter) shows no routing signal without catastrophic failure ($73.5\\%$ vs.\\ $73.0\\%$, $p = 0.81$); Llama-3.1-8B replication ($N{=}220$) confirms death dominance across model families (direction consistency 10/10; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}). Four intervention axes (retry, Reflexion~\\citep{reflexion-2023}, $10{\\times}$ scaling, action-space restriction) reduce death rates but none closes the gap to deterministic safety, with ${\\geq}$12~pp residual gaps (\\S\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}).\nAn exploratory router (Qwen3 LoRA, 0.6B--4B) yields flat step-level balanced accuracy ($56$--$59\\%$); at episode level, only 4B exceeds the always-deterministic baseline (\\S\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).\n\nOur contributions: (1)~an \\textbf{episode-level counterfactual diagnostic framework} that overcomes per-step oracle degeneracy (97.4\\% tie rate), decomposes routing signal via penalty-independent (label fraction) and penalty-dependent (DAF) metrics, and predicts routing value from a ${\\sim}$50-episode pilot (validated 4/4 conditions; Appendix~\\ref{app:decision-framework}); (2)~applying this framework to ScienceWorld, we find that \\textbf{78.9\\% of non-tie routing labels} stem from death asymmetry---a single mechanism that persists across four intervention axes and three model scales, contradicting the routing literature's implicit assumption of diverse signal sources (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\\ref{sec:agent-improvement}); (3)~\\textbf{cross-model validation} (Llama-3.1-8B, DAF${=}88.2\\%$), four intervention axes, and cross-environment boundary probes (MATH, ALFWorld) confirm the framework's diagnostic utility and the robustness of the finding (\\S\\ref{sec:math-contrast}--\\ref{sec:cross-model}).\n\n\n",
